2021	4.3.  NIPC Action APIs

2023	   NIPC Action APIs invoke device actions.  If the underlying protocol
2024	   requires a connection, the gateway establishes it implicitly for the
2025	   operation.  If an explicitly established connection is already
2026	   active, the operation MUST reuse it without modification.

2028	4.3.1.  Perform an action

2030	   Method: POST /devices/{id}/actions{?actionName}

2032	   Description: Perform an action on a specific device

2034	   Parameters:

2036	   *  id: the ID of the device

2038	   Query Parameters:

2040	   *  actionName: the action to perform

2042	   Request Body:

2044	   The request body is optional and may contain a value.  The media type
2045	   of the value can be defined by the underlying protocol, for example
2046	   it could be octet-stream for binary data.

2048	   Response:

2050	   Actions are performed asynchronously.  A successful request returns
2051	   HTTP status code 202 Accepted with a Location header pointing to the
2052	   action instance for status checking, and a Retry-After header
2053	   indicating the number of seconds the client should wait before
2054	   polling.  The Location header contains the URI for the action
2055	   instance, which can be used to check the action status.

2057	   Example of a successful response:

2059	   =============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ================

2061	   HTTP/1.1 202 Accepted
2062	   Location: /devices/1d3b2c36-8a65-45a6-87c1-bcdbe0a32e30/actions?\
2063	                         instanceId=02ee282c-8915-4b2e-bbd2-88966773134a
2064	   Retry-After: 5

2066	4.3.2.  Check action status

2068	   Method: GET /devices/{id}/actions{?instanceId}

2070	   Description: Check the status of an action on a specific device

2072	   Parameters:

2074	   *  id: the ID of the device

2076	   Query Parameters:

2078	   *  instanceId: the instance ID of the action (obtained from the
2079	      Location header)

2081	   Response: MUST return 200 OK with an action status, which may be
2082	   "IN_PROGRESS" or "COMPLETED".

2084	   <CODE BEGINS> file "action_response.cddl"
2085	   ActionResponse = {
2086	     status: ActionStatus
2087	   }

2089	   ActionStatus = "IN_PROGRESS" / "COMPLETED"
2090	   <CODE ENDS>

2092	   Example of a response:

2094	   <CODE BEGINS>
2095	   {
2096	     "status": "COMPLETED"
2097	   }
2098	   <CODE ENDS>

2100	                 Figure 17: Example action status response

2102	4.3.3.  Perform an action on a group of devices

2104	   Method: POST /groups/{id}/actions{?actionName}

2106	   Description: Perform an action on a group of devices

2108	   Parameters:

2110	   *  id: the ID of the group

2112	   Query Parameters:

2114	   *  actionName: the action to perform

2116	   Request Body:

2118	   The request body is optional and may contain a value.  The media type
2119	   of the value can be defined by the underlying protocol, for example
2120	   it could be octet-stream for binary data.

2122	   Response:

2124	   Actions are performed asynchronously.  A successful request returns
2125	   HTTP status code 202 Accepted with a Location header pointing to the
2126	   action instance for status checking, and a Retry-After header
2127	   indicating the number of seconds the client should wait before
2128	   polling.

2130	   Example of a successful response:

2132	   =============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ================

2134	   HTTP/1.1 202 Accepted
2135	   Location: /groups/0dc729d7-f6c3-491d-9b9d-e7176d2be243/actions?\
2136	                         instanceId=02ee282c-8915-4b2e-bbd2-88966773134a
2137	   Retry-After: 5

2139	4.3.4.  Check group action status

2141	   Method: GET /groups/{id}/actions{?instanceId}

2143	   Description: Check the status of an action on a group of devices.
2144	   Returns a per-device status array.

2146	   Parameters:

2148	   *  id: the ID of the group

2150	   Query Parameters:

2152	   *  instanceId: the instance ID of the action (obtained from the
2153	      Location header)

2155	   Response:

2157	   MUST return 200 OK with an array of per-device action statuses.  Each
2158	   entry contains the action status and a deviceId.  Entries for failed
2159	   devices contain a problem detail with the deviceId.

2161	   <CODE BEGINS> file "group_action_status_response_array.cddl"
2162	   =============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ================

2164	   ; Group Action status response array and item shape

2166	   GroupActionStatusResponseArray = [* GroupActionStatusResponse]

2168	   GroupActionStatusResponse = (GroupActionSuccessResponse // \
2169	                                             GroupActionFailureResponse)

2171	   GroupActionSuccessResponse = {
2172	     status: ActionStatus,
2173	     deviceId: text     ; UUID (as text)
2174	   }

2176	   ActionStatus = "IN_PROGRESS" / "COMPLETED"

2178	   GroupActionFailureResponse = {
2179	     FailureResponse,
2180	     ? deviceId: text
2181	   }
2182	   <CODE ENDS>

2184	   Example of a response:

2186	   <CODE BEGINS>
2187	   [
2188	     {
2189	       "status": "COMPLETED",
2190	       "deviceId": "1d3b2c36-8a65-45a6-87c1-bcdbe0a32e30"
2191	     },
2192	     {
2193	       "status": "IN_PROGRESS",
2194	       "deviceId": "d62c7fb2-a216-4811-a388-053b17fdbedc"
2195	     }
2196	   ]
2197	   <CODE ENDS>

2199	              Figure 18: Example group action status response

2201	4.4.  NIPC Trigger APIs

2203	   Triggers APIs do not actually execute an operation on a device or
2204	   group of devices, but install a trigger that registers an operation.
2205	   When triggered the registered operation gets executed.

2207	   Triggers can be installed for devices or groups of devices,
2208	   represented by their id as a path parameter.

2210	   The trigger must always be triggered by an event. the global name of
2211	   the event must be passed as a path parameter.

2213	   The triggered operation must always be an action.  Since an action
2214	   can be executed against both a device and a group, the trigger API
2215	   also supports actions on both devices and groups of devices.  The
2216	   action is defined by its full NIPC URI to be executed, for example:

2218	   =============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ================

2220	   /devices/3171ec43-42a5-4415-ab4b-afd0dfbe9615/actions?actionName=\
2221	          https://example.com/AlarmSystem#/sdfObject/bell/sdfAction/ring

2223	   If a NIPC Gateway supports multiple protocols, then a trigger can be
2224	   defined on a devices that supports one protocol and triggers an
2225	   action on a device that supports a different protocol.  As an
2226	   example, an event on a BLE device can trigger an action on a Zigbee
2227	   device.

2229	4.4.1.  Create a trigger on a device

2231	   Method: POST /devices/{id}/triggers{?eventName}

2233	   Description: Creates a trigger on an affordance of a device.  A
2234	   trigger will trigger an action on another device or a group of
2235	   devices.  If a trigger for the same eventName is already enabled on
2236	   the device, the gateway MUST return an error with the trigger-
2237	   already-enabled problem type.

2239	   Parameters:

2241	   *  id: the ID of the device

2243	   Query Parameters:

2245	   *  eventName: the eventName of the event that is associated with the
2246	      trigger.  This must be a URL encoded string that is the absolute
2247	      URI that is the global name of an sdfEvent.

2249	   Request Body:

2251	   *  The request body must be of type 'application/nipc+json', and
2252	      contain an action object.  The action object contains a NIPC URI
2253	      to be executed, including the device or group id and actionname
2254	      percent-encoded query parameter.

2256	   <CODE BEGINS> file "action.cddl"
2257	   Action = {
2258	     action: text  ; NIPC action operation to execute
2259	   }
2260	   <CODE ENDS>

2262	   Example body for trigger creation:

2264	   <CODE BEGINS>
2265	   =============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ================

2267	   {
2268	     "action": "/devices/3171ec43-42a5-4415-ab4b-afd0dfbe9615/actions?\
2269	   actionName=https%3A%2F%2Fexample.com%2FAlarmSystem%23%2FsdfObject%\
2270	                                              2Fbell%2FsdfAction%2Fring"
2271	   }
2272	   <CODE ENDS>

2274	                    Figure 19: Example trigger creation

2276	   Response:

2278	   *  Returns HTTP status code 201 Created with a Location header
2279	      pointing to the created trigger instance.

2281	   Example of a successful response:

2283	   =============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ================

2285	   HTTP/1.1 201 Created
2286	   Location: /devices/1d3b2c36-8a65-45a6-87c1-bcdbe0a32e30/triggers?\
2287	                         instanceId=02ee282c-8915-4b2e-bbd2-88966773134a

2289	   The Location header must contain the URI for the created trigger
2290	   instance, which may be used to check status or disable the trigger.

2292	4.4.2.  Delete a trigger on a device

2294	   Method: DELETE /devices/{id}/triggers{?instanceId}

2296	   Description: Deletes an installed trigger

2298	   Parameters:

2300	   *  id: the ID of the device

2302	   Query Parameters:

2304	   *  instanceId: the instance ID of the trigger to disable (obtained
2305	      from the Location header when the trigger was installed)

2307	   Response:

2309	   *  Returns HTTP status code 204 No Content on successful delete.

2311	   HTTP/1.1 204 No Content

2313	4.4.3.  Get installed triggers for a device

2315	   Method: GET /devices/{id}/triggers{?instanceId}

2317	   Description: Get the installed triggers for a devices, or a specific
2318	   trigger when specifying an instanceId.

2320	   Parameters:

2322	   *  id: the ID of the device

2324	   Query Parameters:

2326	   *  instanceId: an InstanceID, or comma separated list of event
2327	      instance IDs to filter by (optional)

2329	   *  If no query parameter is supplied, then all triggers for the
2330	      device must be retrieved

2332	   Response:

2334	   The response must be an array of triggers, each containing an
2335	   instanceId, the sdfName of an event and an action.

2337	   *  instanceId: must be the instance ID for each installed trigger, as
2338	      returned by the NIPC GW when the trigger was created.

2340	   *  eventName: must be the sdfName of the event that is associated
2341	      with the trigger.

2343	   *  action: must be the NIPC URI of the action to be executed when the
2344	      trigger is activated.

2346	   <CODE BEGINS> file "trigger_status_array.cddl"
2347	   ; Trigger status response array and item shape

2349	   TriggerStatusResponseArray = [* TriggerResponse]

2351	   TriggerResponse = {
2352	     eventName: text,      ; SDF event name
2353	     action: text,      ; NIPC action operation to execute
2354	     instanceId: text     ; UUID (as text)
2355	   }
2356	   <CODE ENDS>

2358	   Example of a response:

2360	   <CODE BEGINS>
2361	   =============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ================

2363	   [
2364	     {
2365	       "instanceId": "02ee282c-8915-4b2e-bbd2-88966773134a",
2366	       "eventName": "https://example.com/heartrate#/sdfObject/\
2367	                                    healthsensor/sdfEvent/fallDetected",
2368	       "action": "/devices/3171ec43-42a5-4415-ab4b-afd0dfbe9615/actions\
2369	   ?actionName=https%3A%2F%2Fexample.com%2FAlarmSystem%23%2FsdfObject%\
2370	                                              2Fbell%2FsdfAction%2Fring"
2371	     }
2372	   ]
2373	   <CODE ENDS>

2375	             Figure 20: Example get multiple triggers response

2377	4.4.4.  Create a trigger on a group of devices

2379	   Method: POST /groups/{id}/triggers{?eventName}

2381	   Description: Creates a trigger on an affordance of a group of
2382	   devices.  A trigger will trigger an action on another device or a
2383	   group of devices.  If a trigger for the same eventName is already
2384	   enabled on the group, the gateway MUST return an error with the
2385	   trigger-already-enabled problem type.

2387	   Parameters:

2389	   *  id: the ID of the group of devices

2391	   Query Parameters:

2393	   *  eventName: the eventName of the affordance that is associated with
2394	      the trigger.  This must be an event.  The eventName must be a URL
2395	      encoded string that is the absolute URI that is the global name of
2396	      an sdfEvent.

2398	   Request Body:

2400	   *  The request body must be of type 'application/nipc+json', and
2401	      contain an action object.  The action object contains a NIPC URI
2402	      to be executed, including the device or group id and actionname
2403	      percent-encoded query parameter.

2405	   <CODE BEGINS> file "action.cddl"
2406	   Action = {
2407	     action: text  ; NIPC action operation to execute
2408	   }
2409	   <CODE ENDS>

2411	   Example body for trigger creation:

2413	   <CODE BEGINS>
2414	   =============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ================

2416	   {
2417	     "action": "/groups/3171ec43-42a5-4415-ab4b-afd0dfbe9615/actions?\
2418	   actionName=https%3A%2F%2Fexample.com%2FAlarmSystem%23%2FsdfObject%\
2419	                                              2Fbell%2FsdfAction%2Fring"
2420	   }
2421	   <CODE ENDS>

2423	                    Figure 21: Example trigger creation

2425	   Response:

2427	   *  Returns HTTP status code 201 Created with a Location header
2428	      pointing to the created trigger instance.

2430	   Example of a successful response:

2432	   =============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ================

2434	   HTTP/1.1 201 Created
2435	   Location: /devices/1d3b2c36-8a65-45a6-87c1-bcdbe0a32e30/triggers?\
2436	                         instanceId=02ee282c-8915-4b2e-bbd2-88966773134a

2438	   The Location header must contain the URI for the created trigger
2439	   instance, which may be used to check status or disable the trigger.

2441	4.4.5.  Delete a trigger on a group of devices

2443	   Method: DELETE /groups/{id}/triggers{?instanceId}

2445	   Description: Deletes an installed trigger

2447	   Parameters:

2449	   *  id: the ID of the group of devices

2451	   Query Parameters:

2453	   *  instanceId: the instance ID of the trigger to disable (obtained
2454	      from the Location header when the trigger was installed)

2456	   Response:

2458	   *  Returns HTTP status code 204 No Content on successful delete.

2460	   HTTP/1.1 204 No Content

2462	4.4.6.  Get installed triggers for a group of devices

2464	   Method: GET /groups/{id}/triggers{?instanceId}

2466	   Description: Get the installed triggers for a group of devices, or a
2467	   specific trigger when specifying an instanceId.

2469	   Parameters:

2471	   *  id: the ID of the group of devices

2473	   Query Parameters:

2475	   *  instanceId: an InstanceID, or comma separated list of event
2476	      instance IDs to filter by (optional)

2478	   *  If no query parameter is supplied, then all triggers for the group
2479	      of devices must be retrieved

2481	   Response:

2483	   The response must be an array of triggers, each containing an
2484	   instanceId, the sdfName of an affordance and an action.  - deviceId:
2485	   must be the device ID for each installed trigger.  - eventName: must
2486	   be the sdfName of the affordance (event or action) that is associated
2487	   with the trigger.  - action: must be the NIPC URI of the action to be
2488	   executed when the trigger is activated.

2490	   <CODE BEGINS> file "group_trigger_status_array.cddl"
2491	   =============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ================

2493	   ; Group Trigger status response array and item shape

2495	   GroupTriggerStatusResponseArray = [* GroupTriggerResponse]

2497	   GroupTriggerResponse = (GroupTriggerSuccessResponse // \
2498	                                            GroupTriggerFailureResponse)

2500	   GroupTriggerSuccessResponse = {
2501	     eventName: text,      ; SDF event name
2502	     action: text,      ; NIPC action operation to execute
2503	     deviceId: text     ; UUID (as text)
2504	   }

2506	   GroupTriggerFailureResponse = {
2507	     FailureResponse,
2508	     ? deviceId: text
2509	   }
2510	   <CODE ENDS>

2512	   Example of a response:

2514	   <CODE BEGINS>
2515	   =============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ================

2517	   [
2518	     {
2519	       "deviceId": "02ee282c-8915-4b2e-bbd2-88966773134a",
2520	       "eventName": "https://example.com/heartrate#/sdfObject/\
2521	                                            healthsensor/sdfEvent/rate",
2522	       "action": "/devices/3171ec43-42a5-4415-ab4b-afd0dfbe9615/actions\
2523	   ?actionName=https%3A%2F%2Fexample.com%2FAlarmSystem%23%2FsdfObject%\
2524	                                              2Fbell%2FsdfAction%2Fring"
2525	     }
2526	   ]
2527	   <CODE ENDS>

2529	          Figure 22: Example get multiple group triggers response

2531	4.5.  NIPC explicit connection management APIs

2533	   Some protocols do not require explicit connection setup; for those
2534	   protocols, the APIs in this section do not apply.  For protocols that
2535	   do require a connection (e.g., BLE), an NIPC gateway performs
2536	   implicit connection management for individual operations (establish
2537	   on demand; release on completion), so clients ordinarily need not
2538	   manage connections.  Clients MAY choose to explicitly establish and
2539	   retain a connection to perform a sequence of operations that depends
2540	   on intermediate results.  This section specifies APIs for explicit
2541	   connection lifecycle control.  Examples use BLE.

2543	4.5.1.  Protocol Information Object

2545	   The protocol information object is used to define protocol specific
2546	   parameters for connections.  The protocol information object is
2547	   protocol specific and defined in the protocol extensions.  An example
2548	   of where a protocol info object would be used is to return protocol
2549	   specific connection parameters when making connections, for example a
2550	   BLE service map.  An example of a BLE protocol information object is
2551	   shown below.

2553	   This specification defines both the BLE and the Zigbee protocol
2554	   information objects for connections and broadcast messages.

2556	   <CODE BEGINS> file "protocolinfo.cddl"
2557	   =============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ================

2559	   ; Top-level wrappers
2560	   protocol-info-service-map = (
2561	     ? protocolInformation: ble-service-map / zigbee-service-map
2562	   )

2564	   protocol-info-broadcast = (
2565	     ? protocolInformation: ble-broadcast / zigbee-broadcast
2566	   )

2568	   ; BLE protocol information
2569	   ble-service-map = {
2570	     ble: {
2571	       ? services: [* ble-service],
2572	       ? cached: bool,
2573	       ? cacheExpiryDuration: int,
2574	       ? autoUpdate: bool,
2575	       ? bonding: bonding-type,
2576	     }
2577	   }
2578	   bonding-type = "default" / "none" / "justworks" / "passkey" / "oob"

2580	   ble-service = {
2581	     serviceID: uuid,
2582	     ? characteristics: [* ble-characteristic],
2583	   }

2585	   ble-characteristic = {
2586	     characteristicID: uuid,
2587	     ? flags: [* ble-flag],
2588	     ? descriptors: [* ble-descriptor],
2589	   }

2591	   ble-flag = "read" / "write" / "notify" / "indicate" / "write-no-\
2592	                                                               response"

2594	   ble-descriptor = {
2595	     descriptorID: uuid,
2596	   }

2598	   ble-broadcast = {
2599	     ble: {
2600	       ? connectable: bool,
2601	     },
2602	   }

2604	   ; Zigbee protocol information
2605	   zigbee-service-map = {
2606	     zigbee: {
2607	       ? endpoints: [* zigbee-endpoint],
2608	     },
2609	   }

2611	   zigbee-endpoint = {
2612	     endpointID: uint,
2613	     ? clusters: [* zigbee-cluster],
2614	   }

2616	   zigbee-cluster = {
2617	     clusterID: uint,
2618	     ? attributes: [* zigbee-attribute],
2619	   }

2621	   zigbee-attribute = {
2622	     attributeID: uint,
2623	     attributeType: uint,
2624	     ? flags: [* zigbee-flag],
2625	   }
2626	   zigbee-flag = "read" / "write" / "report"

2628	   zigbee-broadcast = {
2629	     zigbee: {
2630	     },
2631	   }

2633	   ; Basic types
2634	   uuid = tstr .regexp "(?i)^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-\
2635	                                                    f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$"
2636	   <CODE ENDS>

2638	4.5.2.  Connect to a device

2640	   Method: POST /devices/{id}/connections

2642	   Description: Connect to a device

2644	   Parameters:

2646	   *  id: the ID of the device

2648	   Request Body:

2650	   *  Connection retry parameters

2652	   *  A protocol info object representing the BLE service map to be
2653	      discovered.  In the case of BLE, if no protocol info object is
2654	      included, service discovery is performed to discover all supported
2655	      properties when connecting to a device.  Optionally, service
2656	      discovery may be limited to properties defined in the "ble"
2657	      protocol extension.  The services to be discovered can be added in
2658	      an array.  Property discovery can be buffered across connections,
2659	      so the API also supports caching parameters.

2661	   <CODE BEGINS> file "connection.cddl"
2662	   ConnectionRequest = {
2663	     ? retries: uint,
2664	     ? protocol-info-service-map
2665	   }

2667	   ConnectionResponse = {
2668	     id: text,  ; Device ID
2669	     ? protocol-info-service-map
2670	   }
2671	   <CODE ENDS>
2672	   Example body of a connection without specific discovery of
2673	   properties:

2675	   <CODE BEGINS>
2676	   {
2677	     "retries": 3
2678	   }
2679	   <CODE ENDS>

2681	                       Figure 23: Example connection

2683	   where-

2685	   *  "retries" defines the number of retries in case the operation does
2686	      not succeed

2688	   In case the application would like to discover specific properties of
2689	   a device, an additional protocol information object can be provided
2690	   that defines what properties should be discovered.

2692	   Example body of a BLE connection with specific discovery of
2693	   properties:

2695	   <CODE BEGINS>
2696	   {
2697	     "retries": 3,
2698	     "protocolInformation": {
2699	       "ble": {
2700	         "services": [
2701	           {
2702	             "serviceID": "00001809-0000-1000-8000-00805f9b34fb"
2703	           }
2704	         ],
2705	         "cached": false,
2706	         "cacheExpiryDuration": 3600,
2707	         "autoUpdate": true,
2708	         "bonding": "default"
2709	       }
2710	     }
2711	   }
2712	   <CODE ENDS>

2714	    Figure 24: Example connection with explicit discovery of connections

2716	   where in the BLE protocol object:

2718	   *  "services" is an array of services defined by their serviceIDs.

2720	   *  "cached" refers to whether the services need to be discovered for
2721	      this connection.  If cached is true, the services will be
2722	      discovered for this connection only if it is not present in the
2723	      cache.  If cached is false, the services will be discovered for
2724	      this connection.  The services will be cached once it is
2725	      discovered.

2727	   *  "cacheExpiryDuration" defines how long (in seconds) the cache
2728	      should be maintained before purging.

2730	   *  some devices support notifications on changes in services,
2731	      "autoUpdate" allows the network to update services based on
2732	      notification (on by default)

2734	   *  "bonding" allows you to override the bonding method configured in
2735	      the device object.  Possible values are default, none, justworks,
2736	      passkey, oob.  Default behavior is to use the bonding method
2737	      defined in the device object.

2739	   Response:

2741	   Success responses includes an optional protocol information object
2742	   with an array of discovered properties, as defined in the BLE
2743	   protocol info object in section Section 4.5.1.  This is an array of
2744	   supported services, which in turn contains an array of
2745	   characteristics, which in turn contains an array of descriptors, as
2746	   shown in Figure 25.

2748	       services
2749	        - serviceID
2750	           |
2751	           |> characteristics
2752	               - characteristicID
2753	               - flags
2754	                  |
2755	                  |> Descriptors
2756	                      - descriptorID

2758	                          Figure 25: BLE Services

2760	   Example of a response:

2762	   <CODE BEGINS>
2763	   {
2764	     "id": "1d3b2c36-8a65-45a6-87c1-bcdbe0a32e30",
2765	     "protocolInformation": {
2766	       "ble": {
2767	         "services": [
2768	           {
2769	             "serviceID": "00001809-0000-1000-8000-00805f9b34fb",
2770	             "characteristics": [
2771	               {
2772	                 "characteristicID":
2773	                     "00002a1c-0000-1000-8000-00805f9b34fb",
2774	                 "flags": [
2775	                   "read",
2776	                   "write"
2777	                 ],
2778	                 "descriptors": [
2779	                   {
2780	                     "descriptorID":
2781	                         "00002902-0000-1000-8000-00805f9b34fb"
2782	                   }
2783	                 ]
2784	               }
2785	             ]
2786	           }
2787	         ]
2788	       }
2789	     }
2790	   }
2791	   <CODE ENDS>

2793	                   Figure 26: Example connection response

2795	   where-

2797	   *  "id" is the ID of the device

2799	   *  "protocolInformation" contains an Array of BLE services as shown
2800	      in Figure 25

2802	   A failure will generate a standard failed response.  Please refer to
2803	   Figure 5 definition of failed response.

2805	4.5.3.  Update a connection

2807	   Method: PUT /devices/{id}/connections
2808	   Description: Update a connection, for BLE this will update the cached
2809	   ServiceMap for a device.  Full service discovery will be performed,
2810	   unless specific services are described in the API body.

2812	   Parameters:

2814	   *  id: the ID of the device

2816	   Request Body:

2818	   *  A protocol information object.  In the case of BLE, if no protocol
2819	      information is included, service discovery is performed to
2820	      discover all supported properties when connecting to a device.
2821	      Optionally, service discovery may be limited to properties defined
2822	      in the "ble" protocol extension.  The services to be discovered
2823	      can be added in an array.  Property discovery can be buffered
2824	      across connections, so the API also supports caching parameters.

2826	   Example body of an update connection:

2828	   <CODE BEGINS>
2829	   {
2830	     "protocolInformation": {
2831	       "ble": {
2832	         "services": [
2833	           {
2834	             "serviceID": "00001809-0000-1000-8000-00805f9b34fb"
2835	           }
2836	         ],
2837	         "cached": false,
2838	         "cacheExpiryDuration": 3600,
2839	         "autoUpdate": true
2840	       }
2841	     }
2842	   }
2843	   <CODE ENDS>

2845	               Figure 27: Example service discovery response

2847	   where in the BLE protocol object:

2849	   *  "services" is an array of services defined by their serviceIDs

2851	   *  "cached" refers to whether the services need to be cached for
2852	      subsequent connects, in order not to perform service discovery on
2853	      each request

2855	   *  "cacheExpiryDuration" defines how long the cache should be
2856	      maintained before purging

2858	   *  some devices support notifications on changes in services,
2859	      "autoUpdate" allows the network to update services based on
2860	      notification (on by default)

2862	   Response:

2864	   Success responses include a protocol information object with an array
2865	   of discovered properties, as defined in the specific protocol.  For
2866	   example, for BLE, this is an array of supported services, which in
2867	   turn contains an array of characteristics, which in turn contains an
2868	   array of descriptors, as shown in Figure 25.

2870	   Example of a response:

2872	   <CODE BEGINS>
2873	   {
2874	     "id": "1d3b2c36-8a65-45a6-87c1-bcdbe0a32e30",
2875	     "protocolInformation": {
2876	       "ble": {
2877	         "services": [
2878	           {
2879	             "serviceID": "00001809-0000-1000-8000-00805f9b34fb",
2880	             "characteristics": [
2881	               {
2882	                 "characteristicID":
2883	                   "00002a1c-0000-1000-8000-00805f9b34fb",
2884	                 "flags": [
2885	                   "read",
2886	                   "write"
2887	                 ],
2888	                 "descriptors": [
2889	                   {
2890	                     "descriptorID":
2891	                       "00002902-0000-1000-8000-00805f9b34fb"
2892	                   }
2893	                 ]
2894	               }
2895	             ]
2896	           }
2897	         ]
2898	       }
2899	     }
2900	   }
2901	   <CODE ENDS>
2902	                   Figure 28: Example connection response

2904	   where-

2906	   *  "id" is the ID of the device

2908	   *  "protocolInformation" contains an Array of BLE services as shown
2909	      in Figure 25 and described in Section 4.5.1.

2911	   A failure will generate a standard failed response.  Please refer to
2912	   Figure 5 definition of failed response.

2914	4.5.4.  Disconnect from a device

2916	   Method: DELETE /devices/{id}/connections

2918	   Description: Disconnect from a device

2920	   Parameters:

2922	   *  id: the ID of the device

2924	   Response:

2926	   Returns HTTP status code 204 No Content on successful disconnect.

2928	   A failure will generate a standard failed response.  Please refer to
2929	   Figure 5 definition of failed response.

2945	   Example of a response:

2947	   <CODE BEGINS>

2955	             "characteristics": [
2956	               {
2957	                 "characteristicID":
2958	                   "00002a1c-0000-1000-8000-00805f9b34fb",
2959	                 "flags": [
2960	                   "read",
2961	                   "write"
2962	                 ],
2963	                 "descriptors": [
2964	                   {
2965	                     "descriptorID":
2966	                       "00002902-0000-1000-8000-00805f9b34fb"
2967	                   }
2968	                 ]

2990	5.  NIPC Extensibility

2992	   NIPC is designed to be extensible in two complementary ways:

2994	   *  Protocol mappings: NIPC relies on SDF protocol mappings to relate
2995	      protocol-neutral affordances to protocol-specific operations.
2996	      Adding a new mapping enables support for additional device
2997	      protocols (or versions) without the need to change the NIPC API
2998	      itself.  This allows deployments to evolve as device ecosystems
2999	      change, while preserving application portability and gateway
3000	      interoperability.  Protocol mapping is an extension to SDF and
3001	      described in [I-D.ietf-asdf-sdf-protocol-mapping].

3003	   *  API extensions: Extensions compose multiple NIPC operations into a
3004	      single request or introduce specialized flows optimized for common
3005	      scenarios (e.g., bulk property updates, conditional reads,
3006	      firmware operations).  Extensions MUST use the “/extensions” path
3007	      element and SHOULD focus on efficiency, latency reduction, and
3008	      operational simplicity (fewer round trips, reduced state
3009	      management).  To ensure interoperability and consistent security
3010	      review, extensions MUST be registered with IANA as defined in
3011	      Section 11.3.1.

3013	5.1.  Protocol mappings

3015	   NIPC relies on SDF protocol mappings
3016	   [I-D.ietf-asdf-sdf-protocol-mapping] to relate protocol-neutral
3017	   affordances to protocol-specific operations.  In
3018	   [I-D.ietf-asdf-sdf-protocol-mapping] BLE and Zigbee are used as
3019	   examples, however the mapping mechanism is extensible; so support for
3020	   additional protocols (or versions) can be added without changing the
3021	   NIPC schema or API.

3023	5.2.  API extensions

3025	   The extension APIs allow for extensibility of the APIs.  Extension
3026	   APIs may leverage the basic NIPC defined APIs and combine them in
3027	   compound statements in order to streamline application operation
3028	   against devices, make operations more expedient and convenient in one
3029	   API call.  An example of this is the bulk API extension.  They may
3030	   also introduce new functionality that is specific to a use case or
3031	   protocol, such as the BLE transmit API.

3033	   Extensions must be defined under the /extensions path element.  The
3034	   extension name is defined as a path parameter after the /extensions
3035	   path element.  Extensions may define their own request and response
3036	   payloads, as well as their own query parameters.  Extensions must be
3037	   IANA registered as defined in Section 11.3.1.

3039	   Extensions MAY implement long-running operations (e.g., firmware
3040	   updates, bulk actions).  For such operations, the server SHOULD
3041	   respond with 202 Accepted and a Location header referencing a status
3042	   URI.  Clients MAY poll the status URI (GET) to obtain progress.
3043	   While the operation is in progress, the status endpoint SHOULD return
3044	   200 OK with status information; upon completion, the server MAY
3045	   redirect with 303 See Other to the final result resource, or return
3046	   200 OK with the completed result from the status endpoint.  This
3047	   pattern minimizes client state, supports retry, and provides a
3048	   uniform mechanism for tracking asynchronous execution.

3050	   In the appendix Appendix D, we have defined a few example extensions.

3052	6.  NIPC Error Handling

3054	   Error types in NIPC APIs must use URI-based error type identifiers as
3055	   defined in Section 11.4.  The error types can be generic or specific
3056	   to the API category.  The error types are organized into the
3057	   following categories:

3059	   *  Generic: Broadly applicable errors, including authorization,
3060	      invalid identifiers, and generic failures.

3062	   *  Property APIs: Errors related to property APIs (read/write).

3064	   *  Event APIs: Errors related to event APIs (enable/disable).

3066	   *  Protocol specific: Errors related to protocol-specific operations.

3068	   *  Extension APIs: Errors related to extension APIs.

3070	   NIPC error types are defined in the table below:

3072	    +======================+==============================+===========+
3073	    | Error Type           | Description                  | Category  |
3074	    +======================+==============================+===========+
3075	    | invalid-id           | Invalid device ID or gateway | Generic   |
3076	    |                      | doesn't recognize the ID     |           |
3077	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3078	    | invalid-sdf-url      | Invalid SDF URL or SDF       | Generic   |
3079	    |                      | affordance not found         |           |
3080	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3081	    | extension-operation- | Operation was not executed   | Generic   |
3082	    | not-executed         | since the previous operation |           |
3083	    |                      | failed                       |           |
3084	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3085	    | sdf-model-already-   | SDF model already registered | Generic   |
3086	    | registered           |                              |           |
3087	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3088	    | sdf-model-in-use     | SDF model in use             | Generic   |
3089	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3090	    | unsupported-uri-     | Unsupported URI scheme       | Generic   |
3091	    | scheme               |                              |           |
3092	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3093	    | property-not-        | Property not readable        | Property  |
3094	    | readable             |                              | APIs      |
3095	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3096	    | property-not-        | Property not writable        | Property  |
3097	    | writable             |                              | APIs      |
3098	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3099	    | property-read-failed | Property read failed         | Property  |
3100	    |                      |                              | APIs      |
3101	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3102	    | property-write-      | Property write failed        | Property  |
3103	    | failed               |                              | APIs      |
3104	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3105	    | event-already-       | Event already enabled        | Event     |
3106	    | enabled              |                              | APIs      |
3107	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3108	    | event-not-enabled    | Event not enabled            | Event     |
3109	    |                      |                              | APIs      |
3110	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3111	    | event-not-registered | Event not registered for any | Event     |
3112	    |                      | data application             | APIs      |
3113	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3114	    | trigger-already-     | Trigger already enabled for  | Trigger   |
3115	    | enabled              | this event                   | APIs      |
3116	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3117	    | protocolmap-ble-     | Device already connected     | Protocol  |
3118	    | already-connected    |                              | specific  |
3119	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3120	    | protocolmap-ble-no-  | No connection found for      | Protocol  |
3121	    | connection           | device                       | specific  |
3122	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3123	    | protocolmap-ble-     | BLE connection timeout       | Protocol  |
3124	    | connection-timeout   |                              | specific  |
3125	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3126	    | protocolmap-ble-     | BLE bonding failed           | Protocol  |
3127	    | bonding-failed       |                              | specific  |
3128	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3129	    | protocolmap-ble-     | BLE connection failed        | Protocol  |
3130	    | connection-failed    |                              | specific  |
3131	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3132	    | protocolmap-ble-     | BLE service discovery failed | Protocol  |
3133	    | service-discovery-   |                              | specific  |
3134	    | failed               |                              |           |
3135	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3136	    | protocolmap-ble-     | Invalid BLE service or       | Protocol  |
3137	    | invalid-service-or-  | characteristic ID            | specific  |
3138	    | characteristic       |                              |           |
3139	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3140	    | protocolmap-zigbee-  | Zigbee connection timeout    | Protocol  |
3141	    | connection-timeout   |                              | specific  |
3142	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3143	    | protocolmap-zigbee-  | Invalid Zigbee endpoint or   | Protocol  |
3144	    | invalid-endpoint-or- | cluster ID                   | specific  |
3145	    | cluster              |                              |           |
3146	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3147	    | extension-transmit-  | Invalid transmit data        | Transmit  |
3148	    | invalid-data         |                              | APIs      |
3149	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+
3150	    | extension-firmware-  | Firmware rollback            | Extension |
3151	    | rollback             |                              | APIs      |
3152	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+

3154	    | update-failed        |                              | APIs      |
3155	    +----------------------+------------------------------+-----------+

3157	                            Table 3: Error Codes

3159	   The appropriate HTTP status code is returned in the response.

3161	7.  Publish/Subscribe Interface

3163	   Events are delivered via a publish/subscribe interface.  Event types
3164	   include: (1) streaming data (enabled/disabled via the NIPC Events
3165	   API), (2) broadcasts (e.g., advertisements), and (3) connection
3166	   status (device link up/down).  Event payloads are encoded in CBOR
3167	   [RFC8949] and MAY be transported over MQTT, webhook, or websocket.
3168	   CBOR is used because non-IP device payloads are typically binary; it
3169	   encodes such data efficiently and is more compact than JSON, reducing
3170	   bandwidth.

3172	7.1.  CDDL Definition

3174	   The event streaming format is defined here in CDDL [RFC8610].  A
3175	   DataSubscription is a CBOR map containing the raw payload (bytes) and
3176	   a timestamp (epoch seconds).  It MAY include deviceID (the SCIM
3177	   device identifier) when the payload is associated with a known
3178	   device.  Optional members (e.g., apMacAddress, rssi) MAY be present
3179	   but can reveal deployment topology and SHOULD be omitted unless
3180	   required.  A choice group within DataSubscription indicates the event
3181	   type (e.g., advertisement, subscription notification, connection
3182	   status).  An event publication MAY carry one or more DataSubscription
3183	   entries in an array.  Such an array is represented as DataBatch.

3185	   The subscription type choice uses a CDDL socket ($$subscription-
3186	   extension) to allow extensibility.  New subscription types registered
3187	   with IANA (see Section 11.3.2) can be added using the CDDL plug
3188	   syntax (//=) without modifying the base schema.

3190	   <CODE BEGINS> file "data_subscription.cddl"
3191	   start = DataBatch

3193	   DataBatch = [* DataSubscription]

3195	   DataSubscription = {
3196	     ? data: bytes,
3197	     timestamp: float, ; epoch in seconds
3198	     ? deviceID: text,
3199	     ? apMacAddress: text,
3200	     subscription
3201	   }

3203	   ; Subscription type - IANA registered types
3204	   subscription = (
3205	     bleSubscription: BleSubscription //
3206	     bleAdvertisement: BleAdvertisement //
3207	     bleConnectionStatus: BleConnectionStatus //
3208	     zigbeeSubscription: ZigbeeSubscription //
3209	     zigbeeClusterCommand: ZigbeeClusterCommand //
3210	     zigbeeConnectionStatus: ZigbeeConnectionStatus //
3211	     $$subscription-extension
3212	   )

3214	   BleSubscription = {
3215	     serviceID: text,
3216	     characteristicID: text
3217	   }

3219	   BleAdvertisement = {
3220	     macAddress: text,
3221	     ? rssi: nint,
3222	   }

3224	   BleConnectionStatus = {
3225	     macAddress: text,
3226	     connected: bool,
3227	     ? reason: int
3228	   }

3230	   ZigbeeSubscription = {
3231	     type: "attribute_reporting" / "write_event",
3232	     endpointID: uint,
3233	     clusterID: uint,
3234	     attributeID: uint,
3235	     attributeType: uint,
3236	     ? profileID: uint,
3237	     ? manufacturerCode: uint
3238	   }

3240	   ZigbeeClusterCommand = {
3241	     endpointID: uint,
3242	     clusterID: uint,
3243	     commandID: uint,
3244	     direction: "client_to_server" / "server_to_client",
3245	     ? profileID: uint,
3246	     ? manufacturerCode: uint
3247	   }

3249	   ZigbeeConnectionStatus = {
3250	     connected: bool,   ; true = joined, false = left the network
3251	     ? reason: int,
3252	     ? rssi: int,       ; received signal strength indicator, in dBm
3253	     ? lqi: uint        ; link quality indicator (0-255)
3254	   }
3255	   <CODE ENDS>

3367	8.  Examples

3369	   This section contains a few examples on how applications can leverage
3370	   NIPC operations to communicate with BLE and Zigbee devices.

3372	8.1.  Property Read/Write

3374	   In this example, we will connect to a device and read and write from
3375	   a property.

3377	   The sequence of operations for this are:

3379	   *  Declare a device instance using the SCIM Interface (out of scope
3380	      of this memo)

3382	   *  Register an SDF model for the device

3384	      ============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ==============

3386	      POST /registrations/models
3387	      Content-Type: application/sdf+json
3388	      Accept: application/nipc+json
3389	      Host: localhost

3391	      { ... }

3393	      HTTP/1.1 201 Created
3394	      content-type: application/nipc+json

3396	      [
3397	        {
3398	          "sdfName": "https://example.com/thermometer#/sdfThing/\
3399	                                                            thermometer"
3400	        }
3401	      ]

3403	      Request Body: JSON object with the SDF model, from Appendix F

3405	   *  Read a property from the BLE device
3406	      ============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ==============

3408	      GET /devices/1d3b2c36-8a65-45a6-87c1-bcdbe0a32e30/properties?\
3409	      propertyName=https%3A%2F%2Fexample.com%2Fthermometer%23%\
3410	                    2FsdfThing%2Fthermometer%2FsdfProperty%2Fdevice_name
3411	      Accept: application/nipc+json
3412	      Host: localhost

3414	      HTTP/1.1 200 OK
3415	      content-type: application/nipc+json

3417	      [
3418	        {
3419	          "property": "https://example.com/thermometer#/sdfThing/\
3420	                                   thermometer/sdfProperty/device_name",
3421	          "value": "dGVzdA=="
3422	        }
3423	      ]

3425	   *  Write to a property on the BLE device

3427	      ============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ==============

3429	      PUT /devices/1d3b2c36-8a65-45a6-87c1-bcdbe0a32e30/properties
3430	      Content-Type: application/nipc+json
3431	      Host: localhost

3433	      [
3434	        {
3435	          "property": "https://example.com/thermometer#/sdfThing/\
3436	                                   thermometer/sdfProperty/device_name",
3437	          "value": "dGVzdA=="
3438	        }
3439	      ]

3441	      HTTP/1.1 200 OK
3442	      content-type: application/nipc+json

3444	      [
3445	        {
3446	          "status": 200
3447	        }
3448	      ]

3450	8.2.  Enabling an Event on a Device

3452	   In this example, we will declare a device instance, and setup an
3453	   advertisement subscription event for that device.

3455	   The sequence of operations for this are:

3457	   *  Declaring a device instance and endpoint app using the SCIM
3458	      Interface (out of scope of this memo)

3460	   *  Register an SDF model for the device

3462	      ============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ==============

3464	      POST /registrations/models
3465	      Content-Type: application/sdf+json
3466	      Accept: application/nipc+json
3467	      Host: localhost

3469	      { ... }

3471	      HTTP/1.1 201 Created
3472	      content-type: application/nipc+json

3474	      [
3475	        {
3476	          "sdfName": "https://example.com/thermometer#/sdfThing/\
3477	                                                            thermometer"
3478	        }
3479	      ]

3481	      Request Body: JSON object with the SDF model, from Appendix F

3483	   *  Register the data app with the event
3484	      ============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ==============

3486	      POST /registrations/data-apps?dataAppId=0927ce7c-b258-4bfa-a345-\
3487	                                                            bcc9f74385b4
3488	      Content-Type: application/nipc+json
3489	      Accept: application/nipc+json
3490	      Host: localhost

3492	      {
3493	        "events": [
3494	          {
3495	            "event": "https://example.com/thermometer#/sdfThing/\
3496	                                         thermometer/sdfEvent/isPresent"
3497	          }
3498	        ],
3499	        "mqttClient": true
3500	      }

3502	      HTTP/1.1 201 Created
3503	      content-type: application/nipc+json

3505	      {
3506	        "events": [
3507	          {
3508	            "event": "https://example.com/thermometer#/sdfThing/\
3509	                                         thermometer/sdfEvent/isPresent"
3510	          }
3511	        ],
3512	        "mqttClient": true
3513	      }

3515	   *  Enable the advertisement event

3517	      ============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ==============

3519	      POST /devices/1d3b2c36-8a65-45a6-87c1-bcdbe0a32e30/events?\
3520	      eventName=https%3A%2F%2Fexample.com%2Fthermometer%23%2FsdfThing%\
3521	                                    2Fthermometer%2FsdfEvent%2FisPresent
3522	      Host: localhost
3523	      Content-Length: 0

3525	      HTTP/1.1 201 Created
3526	      Location: /devices/1d3b2c36-8a65-45a6-87c1-bcdbe0a32e30/events?\
3527	                         instanceId=02ee282c-8915-4b2e-bbd2-88966773134a

3529	   *  Check the status of the event
3530	      ============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ==============

3532	      GET /devices/1d3b2c36-8a65-45a6-87c1-bcdbe0a32e30/events?\
3533	                         instanceId=02ee282c-8915-4b2e-bbd2-88966773134a
3534	      Host: localhost

3536	      HTTP/1.1 200 OK
3537	      content-type: application/nipc+json

3539	      [
3540	        {
3541	          "instanceId": "02ee282c-8915-4b2e-bbd2-88966773134a",
3542	          "event": "https://example.com/thermometer#/sdfThing/\
3543	                                         thermometer/sdfEvent/isPresent"
3544	        }
3545	      ]

3564	      Host: localhost

3574	                                                            thermometer"
3575	        }
3576	      ]
3577	      Request Body: JSON object with the SDF model, from Appendix F

3579	   *  Register the data app with the event

3581	      ============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ==============

3584	                                                            bcc9f74385b4

3590	        "events": [
3591	          {

3594	          }
3595	        ],
3596	        "mqttClient": true
3597	      }

3600	      content-type: application/nipc+json

3602	      {
3603	        "events": [
3604	          {

3606	                                         thermometer/sdfEvent/isPresent"
3607	          }
3608	        ],

3610	      }

3618	                                    2Fthermometer%2FsdfEvent%2FisPresent

3624	                         instanceId=f1b9f26b-21ce-4deb-9d57-289ba7e17cce

3626	   *  Check the status of the event

3628	      ============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ==============

3630	      GET /groups/0dc729d7-f6c3-491d-9b9d-e7176d2be243/events?\
3631	                         instanceId=f1b9f26b-21ce-4deb-9d57-289ba7e17cce
3632	      Host: localhost

3634	      HTTP/1.1 200 OK
3635	      content-type: application/nipc+json

3637	      [
3638	        {
3639	          "event": "https://example.com/heartrate#/sdfObject/\
3640	                                    healthsensor/sdfEvent/fallDetected",
3641	          "deviceId": "1d3b2c36-8a65-45a6-87c1-bcdbe0a32e30"

3664	9.  Implementation Status

3666	   This section is to be removed before publishing as an RFC.

3668	   This section records the status of known implementations of the
3669	   protocol defined by this specification at the time of posting of this
3670	   Internet-Draft, and is based on a proposal described in [RFC7942].
3671	   The description of implementations in this section is intended to
3672	   assist the IETF in its decision processes in progressing drafts to
3673	   RFCs.  Please note that the listing of any individual implementation
3674	   here does not imply endorsement by the IETF.  Furthermore, no effort
3675	   has been spent to verify the information presented here that was
3676	   supplied by IETF contributors.  This is not intended as, and must not
3677	   be construed to be, a catalog of available implementations or their
3678	   features.  Readers are advised to note that other implementations may
3679	   exist.

3681	   According to [RFC7942], "this will allow reviewers and working groups
3682	   to assign due consideration to documents that have the benefit of
3683	   running code, which may serve as evidence of valuable experimentation
3684	   and feedback that have made the implemented protocols more mature.
3685	   It is up to the individual working groups to use this information as
3686	   they see fit".

3755	10.  Security Considerations

3757	10.1.  Payload Encryption Considerations

3759	   Responses to NIPC operations requests may contain sensitive or
3760	   confidential information.  Therefore, application and device
3761	   implementations should consider payload encryption.  NIPC does not
3762	   provide any payload encryption mechanism.  If payload encryption is
3763	   required, it MUST be provided by the underlying device protocol
3764	   (e.g., BLE security modes) or by the transport-layer security
3765	   mechanism (e.g., TLS).

3767	10.2.  TLS Support Considerations

3769	   NIPC MUST run on top of a transport-layer security mechanism such as
3770	   TLS.  When leveraging TLS, the NIPC gateway MUST support TLS 1.2
3771	   [RFC5246] and TLS 1.3 [RFC8446] and MAY support additional transport-
3772	   layer mechanisms.  When using TLS, the client MUST perform a TLS/SSL
3773	   server identity check, per [RFC6125].  Implementation security
3774	   considerations for TLS can be found in [RFC7525].

3776	10.3.  HTTP Considerations

3778	   NIPC runs on top of HTTP and is thus subject to the security
3779	   considerations of HTTP Section 9 of [RFC7230].

3781	10.4.  Authorization Considerations

3783	10.4.1.  API authorization Considerations

3785	   To enable NIPC gateway functions, the network administrator MUST
3786	   authorize applications (e.g., via exchange of tokens or public keys).
3787	   Authorization MAY be role-based.  The following baseline roles are
3788	   RECOMMENDED:

3790	   *  Provisioning: permitted to create and manage device and
3791	      endpoint-app identities via SCIM (typically co-located with the
3792	      gateway).

3794	   *  Control: permitted to invoke NIPC property, action, and event
3795	      APIs.

3797	   *  Data: permitted to receive streamed event data.  Deployments MAY
3798	      further refine authorization at per-API or per-affordance
3799	      granularity.

3801	10.4.2.  Authorization Token/Bearer Token/Cookie Considerations

3803	   When using authorization tokens such as those issued by OAuth 2.0
3804	   [RFC6749], implementers MUST take into account threats and
3805	   countermeasures as documented in Section 8 of [RFC7521].

3807	   Since the possession of a bearer token, Authorization token, or
3808	   cookie MAY authorize the holder to perform NIPC Operations on
3809	   devices, tokens and cookies MUST contain sufficient entropy to
3810	   prevent random guessing attack; for example, see Section 5.2 of
3811	   [RFC6750] and Section 5.1.4.2.2 of [RFC6819].

3813	   As with all NIPC communications, bearer tokens and HTTP cookies MUST
3814	   be exchanged using transport-layer security mechanism such as TLS.

3816	   Bearer tokens MUST have a limited lifetime that can be determined
3817	   directly or indirectly (e.g., by checking with a validation service)
3818	   by the application.  By expiring tokens, applications are forced to
3819	   obtain a new token (which usually involves re-authentication) for
3820	   continued authorized access.  For example, in OAuth 2.0, an
3821	   application MAY use OAuth token refresh to obtain a new bearer token
3822	   after authenticating to an authorization server.  See Section 6 of
3823	   [RFC6749].  As with bearer tokens, an HTTP cookie SHOULD last no
3824	   longer than the lifetime of a browser session.  An expiry time should
3825	   be set that limits session cookie lifetime as per Section 5.2.1 of
3826	   [RFC6265].

3828	   Implementations supporting OAuth bearer tokens need to factor in
3829	   security considerations of this authorization method [RFC7521].
3830	   Implementers also need to consider authentication choices coupled
3831	   with OAuth bearer tokens.  For example, when using OAuth bearer
3832	   tokens with client authentication via client credentials Section 4.4
3833	   of [RFC6749], implementers need to consider the security
3834	   considerations of client authentication via client credentials as
3835	   described in Section 3.2 of [RFC6819].

3837	10.5.  Other Security Considerations

3839	   *  Preventing automated attacks: It is recommended to limit the
3840	      number of requests that any particular application MAY make in a
3841	      period of time.

3843	   *  Logging and monitoring: It is recommended to log and monitor API
3844	      usage to detect potential abuse or attacks.

3846	   *  Input validation: It is recommended to validate all inputs to
3847	      prevent injection attacks.

3849	   *  Error handling: It is recommended to handle errors gracefully
3850	      without exposing sensitive information.

3852	   *  Least privilege: It is recommended to follow the principle of
3853	      least privilege when granting access to resources.

3855	   *  Storage and handling of sensitive data: Credentials MUST NOT be
3856	      stored in clear-text, but MUST be stored using an encrypted
3857	      protection mechanism (e.g., hashing).

3859	11.  IANA Considerations

3861	   This section provides guidance to the Internet Assigned Numbers
3862	   Authority (IANA) regarding registration of values related to NIPC, in
3863	   accordance with [RFC8126].

3865	11.1.  Media Type Registration

3867	   IANA is requested to add the following media type to the "Media
3868	   Types" registry [IANA.media-types].  This document registers the
3869	   "application/nipc+json" media type for messages of the NIPC APIs
3870	   defined in this document carrying parameters encoded in JSON.

3872	   Type name: application

3874	   Subtype name: nipc+json

3876	   Required parameters: none

3878	   Optional parameters: none

3880	   Encoding considerations: Encoding considerations are identical to
3881	   those specified for the "application/json" media type.

3883	   Security considerations: See Section 10 of this document.

3885	   Interoperability considerations: none

3887	   Published specification: This document, the NIPC API specification.

3889	   Applications that use this media type: Applications implementing NIPC
3890	   APIs for IoT device management.

3892	   Fragment identifier considerations: none

3894	   Additional information:

3896	   *  Deprecated alias names for this type: none

3898	   *  Magic number(s): none

3900	   *  File extension(s): none

3902	   *  Macintosh file type code(s): none

3904	   Person & email address to contact for further information: ASDF WG
3905	   mailing list (asdf@ietf.org), or IETF Applications and Real-Time Area
3906	   (art@ietf.org)

3908	   Intended usage: LIMITED USE

3910	   Restrictions on usage: To be used for NIPC APIs as defined in this
3911	   document.

3913	   Author/Change controller: IETF

3915	11.2.  Well-known URIs

3917	   IANA is requested to register the following well-known URI in the
3918	   "Well-Known URIs" registry [IANA.well-known-uris] as defined by
3919	   [RFC8615]:

3921	     +============+===================+==============================+
3922	     | URI Suffix | Change Controller | Specification Document       |
3923	     +============+===================+==============================+
3924	     | nipc       | IETF              | This document, Section 2.5.1 |
3925	     +------------+-------------------+------------------------------+

3927	                                  Table 4

3929	   The well-known URI for NIPC is:

3931	   /.well-known/nipc

3933	11.3.  Registries

3935	   IANA is requested to create a "Non-Internet-connected Physical
3936	   Components (NIPC)" registry group, with the registries defined in
3937	   this section.

3939	11.3.1.  API extensions

3941	   IANA is requested to create a new registry called "NIPC API
3942	   extensions" in the "Non-Internet-connected Physical Components
3943	   (NIPC)" registry group.

3945	   The registration policy for this registry is Specification Required
3946	   [RFC8126].

3948	   The registry must contain following attributes:

3950	   *  Extension URI

3952	   *  Extension name

3954	   *  Description

3956	   *  Openapi model describing the extension.  This model must be
3957	      reviewed by an expert.

4004	11.3.2.  Data Subscription Types

4006	   IANA is requested to create a new registry called "NIPC Data
4007	   Subscription Types" in the "Non-Internet-connected Physical
4008	   Components (NIPC)" registry group.

4010	   This registry tracks the subscription types used in the NIPC publish/
4011	   subscribe interface for streaming event data from devices.  Each
4012	   subscription type defines a specific data format for a particular
4013	   protocol or use case.

4015	   The registration policy for this registry is Specification Required
4016	   [RFC8126].

4018	   The registry must contain the following attributes:

4020	   *  Type Name: The CDDL key name used in the subscription choice

4022	   *  Description: A brief description of the subscription type

4024	   *  CDDL Definition: Reference to the CDDL structure defining the
4025	      subscription data

4027	   *  Reference: Document defining the subscription type

4029	   IANA is requested to register the following initial entries:

4031	   +======================+===========+======================+=========+
4032	   |Type Name             |Description|CDDL Definition       |Reference|
4033	   +======================+===========+======================+=========+
4034	   |bleSubscription       |BLE GATT   |BleSubscription       |This     |
4035	   |                      |notif-     |                      |document |
4036	   |                      |ication/   |                      |         |
4037	   |                      |indication |                      |         |
4038	   +----------------------+-----------+----------------------+---------+
4039	   |bleAdvertisement      |BLE        |BleAdvertisement      |This     |
4040	   |                      |advertise- |                      |document |
4041	   |                      |ment data  |                      |         |
4042	   +----------------------+-----------+----------------------+---------+
4043	   |bleConnectionStatus   |BLE device |BleConnectionStatus   |This     |
4044	   |                      |connection |                      |document |
4045	   |                      |status     |                      |         |
4046	   |                      |changes    |                      |         |
4047	   +----------------------+-----------+----------------------+---------+
4048	   |zigbeeSubscription    |Zigbee     |ZigbeeSubscription    |This     |
4049	   |                      |attribute  |                      |document |
4050	   |                      |report or  |                      |         |
4051	   |                      |write event|                      |         |
4052	   +----------------------+-----------+----------------------+---------+
4053	   |zigbeeClusterCommand  |Zigbee     |ZigbeeClusterCommand  |This     |
4054	   |                      |cluster    |                      |document |
4055	   |                      |command    |                      |         |
4056	   +----------------------+-----------+----------------------+---------+
4057	   |zigbeeConnectionStatus|Zigbee     |ZigbeeConnectionStatus|This     |
4058	   |                      |device     |                      |document |
4059	   |                      |join/leave |                      |         |
4060	   |                      |connection |                      |         |
4061	   |                      |status     |                      |         |
4062	   +----------------------+-----------+----------------------+---------+

4064	                                  Table 6

4066	   The CDDL definitions for these subscription types are provided in
4067	   Section 7.1.

4069	11.3.3.  NIPC Protocols

4071	   IANA is requested to create a new registry called "NIPC Protocols" in
4072	   the "Non-Internet-connected Physical Components (NIPC)" registry
4073	   group.

4075	   This registry tracks the protocols used in the protocolInformation
4076	   object for NIPC connection management and service discovery
4077	   operations.  Each protocol type defines a specific schema for
4078	   protocol-specific information, as defined in Section 4.5.1.

4080	   The registration policy for this registry is Specification Required
4081	   [RFC8126].

4083	   The registry must contain the following attributes:

4085	   *  Protocol Name: The key name used in the protocolInformation object

4087	   *  Description: A brief description of the protocol type

4089	   *  Reference: Document defining the protocol type

4091	   IANA is requested to register the following initial entries:

4093	         +===============+======================+===============+
4094	         | Protocol Name | Description          | Reference     |
4095	         +===============+======================+===============+
4096	         | ble           | Bluetooth Low Energy | This document |
4097	         +---------------+----------------------+---------------+
4098	         | zigbee        | Zigbee               | This document |
4099	         +---------------+----------------------+---------------+

4101	                                 Table 7

4103	11.4.  Problem Details for NIPC APIs

4110	   [RFC8126].

4421	   The following non-normative model is provided for convenience of the
4422	   implementer.

4424	   <CODE BEGINS> file "openapi.yml"
4425	   =============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ================

4427	   # yaml-language-server: $schema=https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-\
4428	                                                               12/schema
4429	   openapi: 3.0.3
4430	   info:
4431	     title: Non IP Device Control (NIPC) API
4432	     description: |-
4433	       This API specifies RESTful application layer interface for
4434	       gateways providing operations against non-IP devices. The
4435	       described interface is extensible. The examples includes
4436	       leverage Bluetooth Low Energy and Zigbee as they are commonly
4437	       deployed.
4438	     termsOfService: http://swagger.io/terms/
4439	     contact:
4440	       email: bbrinckm@cisco.com
4441	     license:
4442	       name: TBD
4443	       url: TBD
4444	     version: 0.22.0
4445	   externalDocs:
4446	     description: NIPC IETF draft
4447	     url: https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-asdf-nipc/
4448	   servers:
4449	     - url: "{gw_host}/nipc/draft-22"
4450	       variables:
4451	         gw_host:
4452	           default: localhost
4453	           description: Gateway Host
4454	   tags:
4455	     - name: NIPC property APIs
4456	       description: |-
4457	         APIs that allow apps to get and update device properties.
4458	         If the underlying protocol requires connection management, it
4459	         will be performed as part of the API call.
4460	     - name: NIPC event APIs
4461	       description: |-
4462	         APIs that allow apps to enable or disable event reporting on
4463	         devices. If the underlying protocol requires connection
4464	         management, it will be performed as part of the API call.
4465	     - name: NIPC action APIs
4466	       description: |-
4467	         APIs that perform actions on devices or groups.
4468	     - name: NIPC trigger APIs
4469	       description: |-
4470	         APIs that install triggers on actions and events for devices
4471	         or groups. A trigger always triggers an action.
4472	     - name: NIPC management APIs
4473	       description: |-
4474	         APIs that manage device connections.
4475	     - name: NIPC registration APIs
4476	       description: |-
4477	         APIs that register sdf models or data applications

4486	         description: |-

4488	           device. If underlying protocol requires a connection to be
4489	           set up, this API call will perform the necessary connection
4490	           management. If a connection is already active for this

4504	           in: query

4507	           required: false

4527	                   be written to the device.
4528	           required: true

4542	             description: Error response
4543	             content:

4545	                 schema:
4546	                   $ref: '#/components/schemas/FailureResponse'

4558	           device, the existing connection will be leveraged without

6428	   The OpenAPI model for SDF protocol mapping is provided in Appendix B
6429	   of [I-D.ietf-asdf-sdf-protocol-mapping].

6437	     title: SDF Protocol Information
6438	     description: |-

6440	       new protocol information schema please add a reference to the \
6441	                                                           protocol info
6442	       for all the schemas in this file.

6695	Appendix D.  NIPC API extensions

6697	   The following OpenAPI models define a few example extensions to the
6698	   NIPC API.

6700	D.1.  NIPC API write binary blob extension

6702	   <CODE BEGINS> file "Extension-Blob.yaml"
6703	   =============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ================

6705	   # yaml-language-server: $schema=https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-\
6706	                                                               12/schema
6707	   openapi: 3.0.3
6708	   info:
6709	     title: Non IP Device Control (NIPC) API write binary blob extension
6710	     description: |-
6711	       Non IP Device Control (NIPC) API write binary blob extension
6712	     termsOfService: http://swagger.io/terms/
6713	     contact:
6714	       email: bbrinckm@cisco.com
6715	     license:
6716	       name: TBD
6717	       url: TBD
6718	     version: 0.22.0

6720	   externalDocs:
6721	     description: NIPC IETF draft
6722	     url: https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-asdf-nipc/
6723	   servers:
6724	     - url: "{gw_host}/nipc/draft-22"
6725	       variables:
6726	         gw_host:
6727	           default: localhost
6728	           description: Gateway Host
6729	   tags:
6730	     - name:  NIPC API extensions
6731	       description: |-
6732	         APIs that simplify application interaction by implementing
6733	         one or more basic APIs into a single API call.

6735	   paths:
6736	   ### Extensions
6737	     /extensions/{id}/properties/blob:
6738	       put:
6739	         tags:
6740	           - NIPC API extensions
6741	         summary: Write a binary blob to a property on a device
6742	         description: |-
6743	           Write a binary blob to a property on a device. Will chunk up
6744	           the binary blob and perform multiple writes. If the
6745	           underlying protocol requires a connection to be set up,
6746	           this API call will perform the necessary connection
6747	           management. If a connection is already active for this
6748	           device, the existing connection will be leveraged without
6749	           modifying it. ID cannot be a group-id.
6750	         operationId: writeBlob
6751	         parameters:
6752	         - name: id
6753	           in: path
6754	           description: The ID of the device. Group ID is not allowed.
6755	           required: true
6756	           schema:
6757	             type: string
6758	             format: uuid
6759	             example: 1d3b2c36-8a65-45a6-87c1-bcdbe0a32e30
6760	         - name: propertyName
6761	           in: query
6762	           description: |-
6763	             The SDF property name that needs to be written to.
6764	           required: true
6765	           schema:
6766	             type: string
6767	             example: "https://example.com/heartrate#/sdfObject/\
6768	                                        thermostat/sdfProperty/firmware"
6769	         requestBody:
6770	           content:
6771	             application/json:
6772	               schema:
6773	                 $ref: '#/components/schemas/Extension-Blob'
6774	           required: true
6775	         responses:
6776	           '204':
6777	             description: Success, no content
6778	           'default':
6779	             description: Error response
6780	             content:
6781	               application/json:
6782	                 schema:
6783	                   $ref: '../NIPC.yaml#/components/schemas/\
6784	                                                       FailureResponse'

6786	   components:
6787	     schemas:
6788	   # Extensions
6789	   ## A binary blob Extension
6790	       Extension-Blob:
6791	         required:
6792	           - blob
6793	         type: object
6794	         properties:
6795	           blob:
6796	             type: string
6797	             format: byte
6798	           chunksize:
6799	             type: integer
6800	   <CODE ENDS>

6887	                   $ref: "../NIPC.yaml#/components/schemas/\
6888	                                                        FailureResponse"

6891	             "{$request.body#/callback.url}":

6899	                         allOf:
6900	                           - $ref: "../NIPC.yaml#/components/schemas/Id"
6901	                           - $ref: "#/components/schemas/Extension-\
6902	                                                           BulkResponse"

6904	                   "200":
6905	                     description: OK
6906	                   "400":
6907	                     description: Bad request
6908	                   "401":
6909	                     description: Unauthorized
6910	                   "405":
6911	                     description: Invalid request
6912	                   "500":
6913	                     description: Server-side failure

6915	       get:
6916	         tags:
6917	           - NIPC API extensions
6918	         summary: Get Bulk response
6919	         description: Get Bulk response
6920	         operationId: getBulkResponse
6921	         parameters:
6922	           - name: id
6923	             in: path
6924	             description: The ID of the device. Group ID is not allowed.
6925	             required: true
6926	             schema:
6927	               type: string
6928	               format: uuid
6929	               example: 1d3b2c36-8a65-45a6-87c1-bcdbe0a32e30

6931	             in: query
6932	             description: Instance ID of the bulk operation
6933	             required: true

6935	               type: string

6940	             description: OK

8226	Appendix E.  NIPC API CDDL Definition

8228	   The following is a combined reference of all NIPC API CDDL
8229	   definitions used in this document.

8231	   <CODE BEGINS> file "combined.cddl"
8232	   =============== NOTE: '\' line wrapping per RFC 8792 ================

8234	   ; This file is auto-generated from individual NIPC API CDDL files

8236	   ; ============================================

8260	   }
